What the scripts of this repository use of it: (a) the profile scripts (`grails-profiles/{base,profile,rest-api}/commands/*.groovy`): `description` with `usage`, `argument`, `flag`, `completer`; `args`, `flag`, `commandLine`; `model`, `render`, `template`, `file`, `resources`, `source`, `projectPath`, `mkdir`; `console`, `addStatus`, `updateStatus`, `error`; `gradle.<task>`, `ant` (`bug-report` only), `config`, `baseDir`, `buildDir`, `profile`, `isServerAvailable`/`waitForStartup` (`run-app`), `System.setProperty`, and classes of `org.grails.cli`; (b) the scripts of plugins (`grails-geb`, `grails-quartz`, `grails-scaffolding`, `grails-spring-security`, the two `dbmigration` modules, `grails-datamapping-core`): `description`, `args`, `flag`, `model`, `render`, `template`, `templates`, `file`, `resource`, `resources`, `source`, `projectPath`, `mkdir`, `copy`, `addStatus`, `updateStatus`, `error`, `config`, `navigateConfig`, `baseDir`, `buildDir`, `grailsConsole`, `executionContext` (handed to a class, `dbm-*`), `addCancelledListener` (`query-tail`), `gradle` (`s2-quickstart`), other scripts as methods (`generate-all`), and the completers of `org.grails.cli.interactive.completers`. No script of the repository uses `notify`, `on`, `argsMap`, `userHome` or `grailsVersion`. Of the profile scripts, slices 3, 4 and 6 replaced `assemble`, `bug-report`, `console`, `dependency-report`, `run-app`, `run-command`, `run-script`, `shell`, `stats`, `stop-app`, `test-app`, `add-property` and `create-script` by compiled commands; `list-plugins` and `plugin-info` (plugin search, section 2.3), the scripts of the `profile` profile (which goes with profiles) and the five `generate-*` scripts of `rest-api` (slice 9) are not this slice's.

Implemented, in three builds. *The host* (`grails-core/src/cli`, package `org.apache.grails.core.cli.script`): `ScriptCommand`, the base class a script is compiled with, and `ScriptCommandRunner`, the main class. The runner is started with `--` and then the name and the arguments, or with the one argument `--grails-args-file=<path>` (`CommandArgumentsFile` of slice 5a, the same limits); it finds the script by the name of the command, first among `src/main/scripts/*.groovy` of the project (the rule of the shell for names; of two files that give one name the first by file name), then as `META-INF/commands/<name>.groovy` or `<Name>.groovy` on its class path; compiles it with the star imports `grails.util` and `grails.codegen.model`; and runs it. The exit code is 0, or 1 when the script returned `false`, threw, was not found or was not compiled, with one line that says why (the stack trace with `-Dgrails.full.stacktrace=true`). A name that is no name of a command is refused without being repeated. `ScriptCommand` has `description` (both forms do nothing: the closure is not called), `args`, `argsMap`, `flag`, `commandLine` (`rawArguments` is the vector as given), `executionContext` (the type of `grails-core-cli`), `model` with the default package of `grails.codegen.defaultPackage`, `render`, `template` and `templates` (`TemplateRendererImpl`: `src/main/templates` of the project, then `META-INF/templates` on the class path, run as Groovy templates as the shell ran them, here in a JVM of the build), the methods of `FileSystemInteraction`, the methods of `ConsoleLogger`, `console` and `grailsConsole`, `config` and `navigateConfig` (`CodeGenConfig` over `application.yml` and `application.groovy`), `baseDir`, `grailsEnv`, `userHome`, `grailsVersion`, `ant` (created when asked for), `on`, `before`, `after`, `notify` with the start and end events of each script, and another script command as a method (`generateController('Book')`), run in the same JVM. `gradle`, `spring` and `profile` throw with one line that says what to do instead; a method that names no script command says that commands of the command line are run from the command line. A script that imports a class of `org.grails.cli` is not compiled, and the message says that the shell is not there. *The task* (`grails-gradle`): `GrailsCliGradlePlugin` registers `runScriptCommand` (`ScriptCommandTask`, a `JavaExec`) in every project with the `java` plugin that has no task of that name. Its class path is `runtimeClasspath` and `grailsCliClasspath` without the output of the project, so nothing is compiled for it; `base.dir` is the project directory; `--arg=<value>` repeated gives the name and the arguments, `--args-file=<path>` a file with them (checked by the task, read by the JVM of the script, not on its command line); none, both, or a file without a name is refused. *The CLI* (`grails-forge-cli`): `ScriptCommandFiles` lists the scripts of the project `ConsoleProject` finds and trusts by the names of the files in `src/main/scripts`. Nothing is opened: the entries of the one directory are read, at most 4096 of them, and of each `.groovy` entry whether it is a regular file without following a link; a link at `src`, `src/main` or `src/main/scripts`, or a real path outside the project, lists nothing and says so; at most 256 scripts are commands; what is no command (a link, a pipe, a directory, a name that is not words of letters and digits or gives more than 64 characters, the second file of one name) is counted in one diagnostic that names no file. The scripts are added by `ApplicationCommands.addTo`, the one registration of slice 5b, after the commands of the catalog file and as the same kind of command (`ApplicationCommandFile.Entry.script`, `ApplicationCommandCommand`): the collision rule, the listing in the help, short forms, completion, the argument file in a private directory and the refusals are that code and not a copy. A script does not take the name of a built-in (by the rule of slice 5b) nor the name or another name of a command the build lists; `help`, `help --json` and typing the name say so and name `gradle runScriptCommand --arg=NAME`. A script that has a name of the table of old commands (slice 9) is the script, as a command the build lists is: the table is asked only for what is no command. `help --json` describes a script with `origin: "application"`, `target: "standalone"`, `registration: "script"` and an empty `description`; `complete` is `false` when the directory could not be listed or had more than is looked at. Running one prints a deprecation line on standard error and runs `runScriptCommand --args-file=<file>`. Covered by `ScriptCommandsSpec` (through `ForgeCli.execute`, the wrapper fixture of slice 5b with a stand-in task; every script of its projects would leave a file behind if it were evaluated, and help, `help --json`, completion, an unknown command, a refusal and a run through the stand-in leave none; hostile file names, links, a pipe, an unreadable file, 10,000 files, an untrusted project above), `ScriptCommandTaskSpec` (TestKit, a stand-in for the runner) and `ScriptCommandRunnerSpec` (`grails-core`, `testCli`: the real host on scripts, templates and a class path of a plugin).

The conversions of item 5:

| Script | State |
| --- | --- |
| Quartz `create-job` | The compiled `create-job` of the CLI, which a project with the `grails-quartz` feature has since before step 4, with the template of the plugin as its golden file. Changed here to the one thing the script did that the command did not: a name that ends with `Job` is taken without it (`NightlyJob` is `NightlyJob.groovy`, not `NightlyJobJob.groovy`), unless `Job` is the whole name of the class, which the script made an empty name of. The command gives `Job` to the name check as what the kind of class adds, so the names of devices are checked against `<Name>Job.groovy`, the file that is written. |
| Geb `create-functional-test` | Converted (revision 43): the script is removed from the plugin, and `create-functional-test` of the CLI is also a command of a project whose record lists `geb-with-testcontainers` or `geb-with-local-browsers`, written from two CLI templates that share the project override `src/main/templates/FunctionalSpec.groovy`. It is a static command keyed on the feature names, as `create-job` is, not a pack artefact. |
| `s2-create-acl-domains`, `s2ui-create-challenge-questions`, `s2ui-override` | Converted (revision 43), with `init-oauth2` and the three scripts of the core plugin: `GrailsApplicationCommand` classes in `src/cli` of their plugins, published in the `-cli` companion of each, that are `StandaloneCommand`s and so run without the application. |
| `dbm-create-changelog`, `dbm-changelog-to-groovy` | Not converted. They hand the `ExecutionContext` of the shell to `ScriptDatabaseMigrationCommand` classes, so they do not run on the host; they become `ApplicationCommand`s in both `dbmigration` modules beside the other `dbm-*` commands. |
| `query-tail` | Converted (revision 43): `QueryTailCommand` in the cli tier of `grails-datamapping-core`, the first `StandaloneCommand`. It is run by `runCommand` without an application context; the summary is still plain text written again, since the build is no terminal. The `console` target is still not built. |

Not done: the script API types are not re-homed one for one. `ScriptCommand` is a new class with the documented surface; `CommandDescription`, `CommandArgument` and `GroovyScriptCommandTransform` stay in `grails-gradle-model` and are not used by the host, so a description is neither shown nor checked. The description of a script is not listed: the CLI reads no file, and the build writes none into the catalog file; a catalog task that compiled the scripts to the phase the transform runs in could. The scripts of plugins (`META-INF/commands/*`) are run by the task by name and are not listed by the CLI, for the same reason and because the catalog file takes only `target: "application"`. YAML commands (`*.yml` of `src/main/scripts` and `META-INF/commands`) are not run. Chaining is to other scripts only: a script cannot run a built-in of the CLI or a command of the catalog, because it runs inside a build; `gradle` is refused for the same reason. `isServerAvailable`, `waitForStartup`, `addCancelledListener` and `isCancelled` are not there. A script has no terminal of its own: it writes through Gradle, and standard input is that of the build. The rule that makes a file name a command name is written twice, in `ScriptCommandFiles` and in `ScriptCommandRunner`, since the CLI does not use classes of the framework's command line tier; both are held to one table of cases, `script-command-names.tsv`, of which each build has a copy among its test resources, by a feature of `ScriptCommandsSpec` and one of `ScriptCommandRunnerSpec`, and each of the two features fails when the copies differ. A project whose Grails Gradle plugin is older than this task lists its scripts and fails to run them with Gradle's "task not found". The real task with the real host was not run against a generated application (slice 9), nor anything on Windows; the plugin scripts named above were read, not run, on the host. A `runScriptCommand` task that a build registered itself before the plugin is kept, with a warning, and is not covered by a spec. Leaves for step 5: `GroovyScriptCommand`, `GroovyScriptCommandFactory`, `ResourceResolvingCommandFactory` and the steps in the shell.

Changed after a review and a run against a real application; where this paragraph and those above differ, this one holds. *Missing methods.* `ScriptCommand.methodMissing` throws `MissingMethodException`, as `GroovyScriptCommand` did, when no script command has the name, and the three properties a script has only in the shell (`gradle`, `spring`, `profile`) are not declared, so they are missing as any other property is. A closure of a script therefore reaches its delegate: `copy { from ...; into ... }`, the nested elements of an Ant task, builders, a delegate that is set, and a script catches the exception. The one line that says what to do instead is written by `ScriptCommandRunner.start` for a `MissingMethodException` or `MissingPropertyException` of a script that nothing caught. Before, every one of these failed with "calls from()"; the scaffolding plugin's own `install-templates` was among them. *One rule for which file is the script.* The host finds a script of the project as the CLI lists it (`ScriptCommandRunner.projectScript`): a regular file, links not followed, in a `src/main/scripts` that is reached through no link and whose real path is in the project, the first in the order of file names where several give the name. A link is never run as a script of the project, also not by `gradle runScriptCommand --arg=NAME`. A second shared table, `script-command-files.tsv`, has the cases; `ScriptCommandsSpec` holds that the CLI lists a name exactly when the table says the build runs a file for it, `ScriptCommandRunnerSpec` that the host runs that file, and both that the copies are identical. *Listing.* A directory with more than 4096 entries has no script commands and one diagnostic (which of them would have been looked at was the order of the directory); more than 256 scripts are cut after sorting by name. *Scripts of plugins* are found among the entries of `META-INF/commands` of each place on the class path by the same name rule, so `XMLImport.groovy` and `dataImport.groovy` in a jar are reachable; a jar without entries for its directories is asked for `<name>.groovy` and `<Name>.groovy` as before. *Messages.* A script that is not compiled is answered with the first error and its line; that the shell is not there is said only for an unresolved class of `org.grails.cli`. A script that returns `false` is answered with one line that says so. With the system property `grails.full.stacktrace` the stack trace of what a script threw follows the line; `ScriptCommandTask` hands the property of the build to the JVM of the script when it runs (not when it is configured, so a reused configuration does not keep it). The CLI does not pass it yet: `-x` in front of a script command needs `-Dgrails.full.stacktrace=true` added to the Gradle arguments where `ApplicationCommandCommand` runs the task. *Console.* The host installs `ScriptConsole`, a `GrailsConsole` that writes whole plain lines (`| message`, `| Error message`, `| Warning message`) and draws no progress, for the time a script runs: through Gradle the cursor handling of `GrailsConsole` came out as `|message` and as `Error |` on a line of its own. *Arguments are data.* The shell's parser sets the system property `grails.env` when it reads `-Dgrails.env=NAME` (`DefaultCommandLine.setEnvironment`), which in the shell was the command line of the shell itself. In the host the property is put back after the arguments are read: the script sees the argument in `commandLine.systemProperties` and runs in the environment of the build. `--` does not end the flags, as in the shell; `commandLine.rawArguments` has every argument as given. *Class path.* "Nothing is compiled for it" was wrong for a project that depends on another project: the project itself is not compiled, the projects on its `runtimeClasspath` are built (`ScriptCommandTaskSpec` has the case). *Run on the host*, in `ScriptCommandRunnerSpec`, from the sources of the plugins of this repository laid out as their jars have them, against a scratch project: `install-templates`, `create-scaffold-controller`, `create-scaffold-service` and `generate-scaffold-all` of `grails-scaffolding`, `create-job` of `grails-quartz`, `create-functional-test` of `grails-geb`, `s2-quickstart`, `s2-create-persistent-token` and `s2-create-role-hierarchy-entry` of the Spring Security plugin, `s2-create-acl-domains`, `s2ui-create-challenge-questions` and `init-oauth2` run and write their files; `generate-all`, `generate-controller`, `generate-async-controller` and `generate-views` of `grails-scaffolding` are not compiled, because they import a completer of the shell. `s2ui-override` was not run to its end: it renders the views of the plugin, which the build of the plugin puts among its templates and the sources do not have there. The `dbm-*` and `query-tail` scripts are in `src/cli/resources` of their modules and were not run. Not done: nothing here was run through the real Gradle task against a generated application by this change.

Completed after that, by plugin; the table "Script commands of plugins" in section 3 has every script of the repository with where it stands. The four `generate-*` scripts of `grails-scaffolding` name the completer of the shell in full inside their `description`, as `create-scaffold-controller` did, and import nothing: the host does not evaluate a description, so `completer` needs nothing there, and the shell reads the scripts as before. They run on the host against a project with a domain class (the name of the class is given with its package, as `source()` asks for in the shell too; `*` is found from the working directory, which is the project where the task runs). `s2ui-override` works as it is once the plugin is laid out as its build lays it out, with `grails-app/views` under `META-INF/templates/views`. `dbm-create-changelog` and `dbm-changelog-to-groovy` are application commands in both `dbmigration` modules, and `ScriptDatabaseMigrationCommand`, the scripts and the dependency of the two command line tiers on `grails-shell-cli` are gone; two defects of `dbm-changelog-to-groovy` went with them (it wrote to the standard output whatever file it was given, because a `File` that does not exist is false to Groovy 5, and it failed on a YAML changelog, whose parser was given no parameters). `query-tail` stays a script: what `run-app --query-tail` of the CLI does is turn the capture on, and showing the capture is the script's. It no longer imports `CommandCancellationListener`; its run ends with the JVM, and in the shell also when the shell says so. Through Gradle the summary is not redrawn in place, since the console there is no terminal, so `--once` and `--stream` are the forms that read well; a command with a terminal of its own is still the `console` target that does not exist. `ScriptCommandTask` takes the `grails.` system properties of the build when it runs (`GrailsSystemPropertyArgumentProvider`). Not done: the CLI lists no script of a plugin, so each is typed as `gradle runScriptCommand --arg=NAME`; a line for `query-tail` in the table of old commands is the CLI's.

**Launcher and lazy start, as implemented (step 4, slice 8).** Implemented: the Forge application, which has the generator and the features, is no longer started for the command line. `ForgeCli` builds the picocli command line first, on a `ForgeContext` that starts the application the first time something asks for it and closes it when it was started. `GrailsPicocliFactory` makes every command without it: a command that generates (`create-app`, `create-webapp`, `create-restapi`, `create-plugin`, `create-web-plugin`, `feature add`) has a constructor for the `ForgeContext` and asks it for `ProjectGenerator`, `ContextFactory` and its `*AvailableFeatures` when it runs; the other commands and the version provider need nothing; the completion candidates of `--features` are an `Iterable` that asks the application when it is iterated. The code generation commands and `CodeGenConfig` need the application only for a record of the oldest kind, one that names a `profile`, whose features are the defaults the generator computes. So `--help`, `-h`, `help [command]`, `help --json`, `--version`, the refusal of an unknown command, a usage error, `gradle`, the Gradle built-ins, the lifecycle commands, `stats`, `bug-report`, the commands of the application and the code generation commands start no application context; `create-*` (run, `--help`, `--list-features`), `feature add` and completion of a `--features` value do. Decided for the candidates: they stay live and lazy, and are not a list written at build time, because the features an application can have are what the running generator says (its packs included), and a second list would have to be kept equal to it; the price is that the help of a command that creates starts the generator, since it lists the features. Two descriptions are written without the list so that they need no generator, and say `those the option --list-features lists` in its place: the description of `--features` in `help --json`, which is thereby the same whether or not the generator runs, and the usage shown after a mistake in the command line of a command that creates. When the application cannot be started, a command that needs it fails with exit code 1 and one line (`The project generator of the command line could not be started (<exception>: <first line>). ...`, the cause through `FeatureSelectionException.shown`), the start is not tried again by that context, the help of a command that creates is shown with no values, and everything else works: this is the part of the exit criterion "built-in help survives broken application builds" that concerns the CLI's own application; a broken build of the user's application was never in the way of the help, which runs no Gradle.

Measured with the launcher jar (`grails-cli-9.0.0-SNAPSHOT-all.jar`, `org.grails.forge.cli.Application`, JDK 21, macOS arm64, wall time of the process, median of 9 runs after one that is not counted, before and after measured alternately in the same minutes): on a machine that was busy with other builds, so the numbers compare and are not absolute.

| Command | Before | After | Starts the generator |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `--help` | 1.67 s | 0.59 s | no |
| `help` | 1.43 s | 0.50 s | no |
| `help --json` | 1.53 s | 0.57 s | no |
| `--version` | 1.46 s | 0.52 s | no |
| `compile` outside a project (refused, exit 1) | 1.28 s | 0.46 s | no |
| `no-such-command` (exit 2) | 1.34 s | 0.49 s | no |
| `create-app` without a name (exit 2) | 1.20 s | 0.43 s | no |
| `cA --help` | 1.36 s | 1.47 s | yes, for the list of features |
| `create-app --help` | 1.19 s | 1.17 s | yes, for the list of features |
| `create-app example.shop` | 1.40 s | 1.40 s | yes |

`org.apache.grails.cli.GrailsCli` on the same jar measured the same as `Application` within 0.05 s in every row.

The entry point is `org.apache.grails.cli.GrailsCli` in `grails-forge/grails-cli`, beside `DelegatingShellApplication`, so it is in the artifact `org.apache.grails:grails-cli` and in the launcher jar: `main(String[])` ends the JVM with the exit code, `execute(String...)` returns it (0 done, 2 a mistake in the command line, 1 any other failure), and its documentation is the contract for IDEs and scripts. Started through it the command line is called `grails` in its usage texts, and without a command it starts the interactive console in a Grails project and shows the help elsewhere. `org.grails.forge.cli.Application.main` goes through the same `ForgeCli.launch` and keeps what it did: the name `grails-forge-cli` and the interactive shell in every directory. Two options are options of the command line itself, declared on the root command and inherited by every command (picocli `ScopeType.INHERIT`), so they are accepted in front of the command and after its name, are in every usage text and in `help --json`, which has a new member `options` at the top for those of the root (format still 1). `--plain-output`: one switch, `PlainOutput`, sets the system property `picocli.ansi=false` while the command line runs, which everything the CLI writes follows because it all goes through `Help.Ansi.AUTO` (usage texts, `| Error` and `| Warning`, what the commands report, the prompt of the shell), and `GradleRunner` puts `--console=plain` in front of the arguments of Gradle unless they name a console. It is read from the arguments before anything is written, so a refusal is plain too. Without the option nothing changed: picocli writes plain text where the output is no terminal and where `NO_COLOR` is set. `--non-interactive`: without a command the help is shown with exit code 0 and the shell is not started; `shell` is refused with exit code 2. Nothing else in the CLI waits for input. The options are not options of `gradle` or of a command of the application, which give everything after their name away, and nothing after `--` is one. In a project whose `gradle.properties` has a `grailsVersion` of an earlier major version and which has no `.grails/project.yml`, a command that reads the project first warns that this is the command line of Grails 9 (`EarlierGrailsProject`), once per `ForgeContext`, and in `help --json` as a diagnostic; the command then runs.

Verified by `LauncherSpec` (through `ForgeCli.execute` and `ForgeCli.launch` on a context that counts its starts and one that cannot be started; and in a JVM of its own, through `ForkedCli`, that `--help`, `help --json`, `--version`, `--non-interactive`, `compile` and `gradle` load neither `SpringApplication` nor `GrailsApp` while `create-app --list-features` loads both) and `GrailsCliSpec` in `grails-cli` (the entry point on the class path of the artifact).

Not done (the start script, `-t/--type` and `GRAILS_PREFERRED_SHELL`, which were here, are slice d1 of step 5, below): `--offline` and `--refresh` are options of the commands that create an application, not of the command line as Appendix A has them. The guidance in an earlier project is a warning; there is no `project migrate` to point to, and a project on Grails 9 without `.grails` is not told apart from one that was generated by it. The help of a command that creates still starts the generator. The completion script and the man pages are generated with the name `grails-forge-cli`. `console` is not refused with `--non-interactive`: it opens a window and reads nothing from the terminal. The interactive console was not started in a specification.

Implemented, after a second review of the launcher; where this paragraph and the ones above differ, this one holds. *`--non-interactive`.* Every build the command line starts with it is given an input that has ended (`/dev/null`, `NUL` on Windows) in place of the input of the command line, and the environment variable `GRAILS_NON_INTERACTIVE=true`, which a command or a script of the application can read (`GradleRunner`, told by the execution strategy of the command line for the time a command runs). `console` is refused with it, with exit code 2, as `shell` is. After the name of a command of the application, and after the name of the command `run-command` runs, `--non-interactive` and `--plain-output` are that command's arguments. *`GrailsCli.execute` in a JVM of the caller.* When it returns, the system properties that starting the generator set or changed (`grails.env` among them) are put back unless the caller changed them again (`ForgeContext.close`), the generator is started without a shutdown hook, and `picocli.ansi` is put back only while it still has the value `--plain-output` gave it. The interactive shell does not call `System.exit` there: on the interrupt of the terminal outside a build it ends when the command that runs has ended, and `execute` returns 130; in the JVM of the `grails` program it ends the JVM as before. The shell asks under the name the command line was started under, takes `exit` with spaces around it, and ends its last line when the input ends at the prompt. *Commands of a project, outside one.* The class `ProjectOnlyCommand` is gone: where the record of no project was read, the commands that write into a project are the commands themselves, hidden from the list of `help`. They are found by their short forms, show their help and their version, answer a mistake in their use with exit code 2, and when they are run say, with exit code 1 and before anything is said of what is missing, the line every command of a project says there. `gradle`, `run-command`, `run-script` and `feature add` without what they need say that line first as well, and a command says it by the name that was typed (`war`). *A project of an earlier version.* `EarlierGrailsProject` reads `grailsVersion` as Gradle does: from the `gradle.properties` of the project, else from those of the directories above it up to the one with `settings.gradle` or `.git`, and of several in one file the last. Only the commands that write into the project what this version has, and `help`, warn; the commands that run the build or look at the project say nothing. The warning, and what reading the record of a project has to say, go to standard error (`BaseCommand.warned`). *Start.* `VersionInfo.getGrailsVersion` reads the version as a text property and `RunAppCommand` holds a lambda where it held a closure coerced to a function: both went through Groovy's formatting of an object and cost about 70 ms of every start. *A generator that cannot be started.* Spring's report of the failure is not logged before the one line of the command (`simplelogger.properties` of the launcher). Covered by `LauncherSpec`, `CommandCatalogSpec`, `ApplicationCommandsSpec`, `GradleCommandSpec` (the shell on a terminal of streams) and `GrailsCliSpec` in `grails-cli`.

Not done, after the second review: that the 70 ms are gone is measured by hand and held by no specification. The stand-ins are not in `help --json` outside a project. `--non-interactive` does not refuse `run-app` in the foreground, which reads nothing. The shell under `execute` was run on a terminal of streams only.

Implemented, slice d1 of step 5: the `grails` command starts the new entry point and nothing starts the Grails shell. *The launcher.* The start class of `grails-cli`, in the manifest of its jar and of the launcher jar (`Main-Class`) and in the `grails` start script of the distribution, is `org.apache.grails.cli.GrailsCli`. `DelegatingShellApplication` was deleted without a forwarder here; since revision 44 a class of that name is the step by which a wrapper of Grails 7 or 8 updates itself. `GRAILS_PREFERRED_SHELL` is not read. `-t` or `--type` as the first argument is refused by `GrailsCli.execute` with exit code 2 and two lines on the standard error (`grails has no option -t since Grails 9: it chose between the Grails shell and Grails Forge, and there is one command line now. Give the command without it.` and the line that points to `help`); nothing is run, and after the name of a command the option is that command's. *The distribution.* `bin` has `grails` and `grails.bat`; the tasks `createShellCliStartScripts` and `createForgeCliStartScripts` and their scripts `grails-shell-cli` and `grails-forge-cli` are gone. `grails-cli` and `grails-cli-shadow` depend on `grails-forge-cli` and declare `org.slf4j:slf4j-simple` themselves (`runtimeOnly`), with `slf4j-nop` of `grails-forge-cli` excluded as before; `grails-shell-cli`, `spring-boot-cli`, `groovy-ant`, `groovy-templates`, `grails-core` and `grails-url-mappings` are no longer declared, and `grails-cli` compiles against `spring-context` (`compileOnly`) because the methods of `ForgeCli` name its types. The launcher jar is 32.7 MB (52.1 MB with the shell). Without `grails-url-mappings` the launcher has no `grails-web-common`, so `grails.util.Holders` has no holder of servlet contexts, and `Holders.reset()`, which the generator runs when it is closed, threw a `NullPointerException` that was logged as a warning with its stack trace after every `create-app`; `reset()` now leaves the servlet context alone when there is no holder (`HoldersSpec` in `grails-core`, and `GrailsCliSpec` holds that a `create-app` writes nothing to the standard error). *The wrapper.* `grails-wrapper` does not have the name of the start class compiled in: `grails.init.StartClass` reads `Main-Class` from the manifest of the `grails-cli` jar that was downloaded and uses it when it is a class name (letters, digits, `_`, `$` and dots, at most 512 characters) and the jar has that class; otherwise it uses `org.apache.grails.cli.GrailsCli`. The class is loaded from a class loader of the jar and refused when it was not defined by it (a name the wrapper or the JDK also has), and a jar without the start class is refused with one line that names the file. The jars of Grails 7 and 8 name `DelegatingShellApplication` in their manifest, so the wrapper of Grails 9 starts them too (run against the 7.1.4 jar). (As it was until revision 44, since when the class is there again and the wrapper updates itself.) Upgrade note 37 and the upgrade skill give the replacement by hand. *The context class loader.* `DelegatingShellApplication` set the context class loader of the thread to its own before it started a command line, and the generator finds its auto-configurations through it; without it `create-app` through the wrapper failed with `No auto configuration classes found` (found by `verify-wrapper-distribution.sh`).
