### Step 5 — cutover and removals (9.0.x)

After steps 1–4 and their documentation are ready, remove profile modules/plugins, profile resolution and BOM constraints, old dispatcher/entry point/alias scripts and the Spring Boot CLI port. Re-home remaining supported services and script types before deleting their old artifact. Stop emitting `grails.profile`, the profile dependency and `grails-forge-cli.yml`; remove active-profile injection after its migration fixtures pass. Update release/distribution scripts, SDKMAN packaging and project wrapper entry points. Ship the coordinated IntelliJ support with 9.0.0.

The removed `profile` configuration must not continue resolving artifacts. Supply an actionable migration diagnostic for old declarations, including direct Gradle usage; choose and test a rejection-only diagnostic mechanism during implementation rather than assuming Gradle's missing-method exception will contain the upgrade guide. That mechanism is not a supported dependency configuration.

**Exit:** no production build/BOM/distribution dependency on `org.apache.grails.profiles` or the retired shell artifact; migration fixtures and historical docs may still name them. A migrated 8.1 app builds/runs on 9.0, an unmigrated one receives guidance, and the 8.1 distribution retains its original behavior. Measured startup and release artifact verification pass.

The exit criteria after slice b, the last slice that changes code. Met: no build of the repository (root, `build-logic`, `grails-gradle`, `grails-forge`, `end-to-end`), none of the five Grails BOMs, no publish list and no part of the distribution has a dependency on `org.apache.grails.profiles` or on `grails-shell-cli`, and neither is built or published; the Grails Gradle plugins resolve no profile, publish no profile plugin and have no class of the shell; and a build that was not migrated receives guidance: one that still declares a `profile` dependency fails with a message that says to remove the line and names upgrade note 39, and one that still has the wrapper of Grails 7 or 8 is told by note 37 what it sees and what to replace. What still names the profiles or the shell is the record of former commands, the upgrade notes and the upgrade skill, `RENAME.md` with its script, the `grails-shell-cli` purl of the dependency graph file, the diagnostic itself and tests that hold that they are absent. Not established by any slice: a migrated 8.1 application built and run on 9.0 as a fixture (the applications the Forge suites generate and build are 9.0 applications), and the 8.1 distribution, which this branch does not build. Not run: a measurement of startup, and the release artifact verification, which was changed for the removed jar (`verify-jar-artifacts.sh`) and has not run against a staged release.

#### Slices

The cut is based on a scan of the tree at `7f7d4ede58` for `profile`, `Profile`, `grails-shell-cli`, `DelegatingShellApplication`, `grails-forge-cli.yml`, `org.apache.grails.profiles`, `grails-profile`, `GRAILS_PREFERRED_SHELL`, `-t`/`--type` and the packages of the shell (`org.grails.cli`), in the root build, `build-logic`, `grails-gradle`, `grails-forge`, `grails-doc`, `etc` and `.github`. The line numbers of section 4 were not used.

**What the scan found that section 4 does not have.**

- *`grails.profile` has a reader at run time, and two things hang on it.* `GrailsApplicationPostProcessor.setApplicationContext` (`grails-core`) adds the value, `web` when there is none, to the active Spring profiles. `DefaultGrailsPlugin.isEnabled(String[])` enables a plugin that declares `profiles` only when one of them is among the active profiles it is given (`GrailsApplicationPostProcessor`, `GrailsEarlyPluginRegistrationPostProcessor`, `DefaultGrailsPluginManager`), and sixteen plugin classes of this repository declare `def profiles = ['web']`: Quartz, SiteMesh 3, JSON views, markup views, GORM GraphQL, the ten of Spring Security, and `end-to-end/legacy-commands-plugin`. Whether the injected profile is among the profiles at each of the three places was not established. An application without the key runs with `web` where a REST API application or a plugin runs with `rest-api`, `plugin` or `web-plugin` now. The key, the row of the welcome page that shows it and `def profiles = ['web']` of a generated web plugin therefore stay in generated output until slice g. Some 115 configuration files of the repository outside Forge and the profiles have the key (94 under `grails-test-examples`), and Forge's own web application has `grails.profile: rest-api`.
- *The `profile` configuration resolves a profile that nobody declared.* `GrailsGradlePlugin.configureProfile` gives the configuration a default dependency: `org.apache.grails.profiles:<grails.profile system property, or web; web-plugin in a plugin>` in the version of Grails. Nothing but the tooling model (`GrailsClasspathToolingModelBuilder`), which only the shell asks for, resolves it. A generated build without the line builds as before.
- *Who compiles or runs against `grails-shell-cli`*, besides `grails-forge/grails-cli` and `grails-cli-shadow`:

| Module | Edge | What it takes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `grails-data-hibernate5/boot-plugin`, `grails-data-hibernate7/boot-plugin` | `compileOnly` and `testImplementation project(':grails-shell-cli')` | `GormCompilerAutoConfiguration extends org.grails.cli.compiler.CompilerAutoConfiguration`, registered in `META-INF/services/org.grails.cli.compiler.CompilerAutoConfiguration`: a hook of the Spring Boot CLI port (97 files under `org/grails/cli/{archive,boot,command,compiler,groovy,util}`), whose only caller is the `run` command of the shell outside a project |
| `grails-data-hibernate5/dbmigration`, `grails-data-hibernate7/dbmigration` | `cliImplementation(project(':grails-shell-cli'))` | `ScriptDatabaseMigrationCommand` and its spec use `org.grails.cli.profile.ExecutionContext`; the scripts `dbm-create-changelog` and `dbm-changelog-to-groovy` run on it |
| `grails-datamapping-core` | `testCliImplementation(project(':grails-shell-cli'))` | `QueryTailCommandScriptSpec` (`GrailsCli`, `Command`, `ProjectContext`, `GroovyScriptCommandFactory`, `GroovyScriptCommand`, the resource resolvers); the script `META-INF/commands/query-tail.groovy` imports `org.grails.cli.profile.CommandCancellationListener` |
| `grails-scaffolding` | none in the build | seven scripts under `src/main/scripts` name `org.grails.cli.interactive.completers.DomainClassCompleter`; they are not compiled by the build and do not compile on the script host of step 4 |
| `grails-gradle/model` | the other way: the shell compiles against it | `GrailsClasspath.getProfileDependencies`, `org.grails.cli.profile.CommandDescription` and `CommandArgument`, `GroovyScriptCommandTransform`. The script host in `grails-core/src/cli` uses none of them, so the transform has no user once the shell and `ProfileCompilerTask` are gone, which section 4 did not expect |

  `grails-forge-cli` and the script host in `grails-core/src/cli` import nothing of the shell or of a profile. What the launcher gets through the shell without naming it is the SLF4J binding (`slf4j-simple`; `grails-cli` excludes `slf4j-nop` of `grails-forge-cli` for that reason, and `simplelogger.properties` of the CLI is written for it).
- *The wrapper names the launcher class.* `grails-wrapper` loads `org.apache.grails.cli.DelegatingShellApplication` by name from the `grails-cli` jar it downloads (`GrailsWrapper.java`). The wrapper jar is committed in every application, so an application that moves its `grailsVersion` to 9.0 and keeps its wrapper asks the 9.0 `grails-cli` for that class. Settled in slice d1 as no forwarder and a replacement by hand, and changed in revision 44: the wrapper updates itself (upgrade note 37), and the wrapper of 9.0 reads the class from the manifest of the jar.
- *Who reads files of the profile modules at build time.* `grails-test-examples/{gsp-compile-static,welcome-page,welcome-page-jetty}` copy `grails-profiles/web/skeleton/grails-app/views/index.gsp`; `grails-spring-security/{acl,cas}/plugin` declare `profile project(':grails-profiles-web-plugin')`; `grails-test-examples/{issue-11102,issue-views-182,scaffolding}` and four builds under `graphql` declare a profile dependency; `grails-spring-security/rest/testapp-profile` (66 files) applies the `grails-profile` plugin and depends on `:grails-profiles-base`, and only `rest/generate-test-apps.sh` uses it. Forge's `GrailsGspSpec` compared its templates with the skeletons until slice a.
- *Counts.* `grails-shell-cli` 242 files, `grails-profiles` 239; the profiles chapter 13 pages and sixteen lines of `toc.yml`; 46 command reference pages; some thirty other pages of the guide name the shell, `-t`, a profile artifact or `GRAILS_PREFERRED_SHELL`.

**The slices.** The letters are those of the areas the cut was asked for, with one more, g, for the reader the audit found. Each slice leaves every build of the repository green.

- **a. Forge stops emitting what profiles needed** — done, see "Implemented, slice a of step 5" in section 2.4. Files: `grails-forge-core` (the generator, `ProjectStateRecorder`, the feature `GrailsProjectSettings`, the scope enums), the specs of `grails-forge-core`, `grails-forge-cli` and `grails-forge-web` that read the earlier record of a generated application, and the passages of the guide about that record. Depends on nothing. Verified by the Forge suites.
- **d1. Launcher and distribution** — done, see "Implemented, slice d1 of step 5" in section 2.5. As cut: `grails-forge/grails-cli/**`, `grails-forge/grails-cli-shadow/**`, `grails-wrapper/**`, `etc/bin/verify-cli-distribution.sh`, `etc/bin/verify-wrapper-distribution.sh`, the `:grails-shell-cli:installDist` arguments in `.github/workflows/gradle.yml`, `INSTALL`, `RELEASE.md`, `README.md`. The start class of `grails-cli` and of the SDKMAN distribution becomes `org.apache.grails.cli.GrailsCli`; the `grails-shell-cli` and `grails-forge-cli` start scripts, `-t`/`--type` and `GRAILS_PREFERRED_SHELL` go; `grails-cli` and the shadow jar drop `grails-shell-cli` and the dependencies their build files list for it (`spring-boot-cli`, `groovy-ant`, `groovy-templates`, `grails-core`, `grails-url-mappings`), and declare the SLF4J binding themselves. Decision to take first: whether a class named `DelegatingShellApplication` stays as a forwarder to `GrailsCli` for the wrapper jars that applications have committed, or the upgrade notes tell every application to take the new wrapper. Depends on the commands of section 3 that have no replacement being accepted as they are. Verified by `GrailsCliSpec`, `LauncherSpec`, the two verify scripts and a measured start. After it the shell is built and published and nothing starts it.
- **d2. Consumers leave the shell** — four parts with no file in common, for four people: (i) the two `boot-plugin` modules: `GormCompilerAutoConfiguration`, its service file, the two build lines and `grails-data-hibernate7/README.md`; decision to take first: delete the class with the `run` command of the shell, which is what calls it, or re-home `org.grails.cli.compiler` with a command that runs a script outside a project; (ii) the two `dbmigration` modules: `ScriptDatabaseMigrationCommand`, its spec, the two scripts made `ApplicationCommand`s, the `cliImplementation` line; (iii) `grails-datamapping-core`: `query-tail.groovy`, `QueryTailCommandScriptSpec`, the `testCliImplementation` line; (iv) `grails-scaffolding/src/main/scripts`: the seven scripts, of which the plugin has compiled commands of the same names. Depends on nothing. Verified per module by its tests and by no `org.grails.cli` being named outside `grails-shell-cli`, `grails-profiles` and `grails-gradle`.
  Implemented, slice d2(i) of step 5: the decision is to delete. `GormCompilerAutoConfiguration` of `grails-data-hibernate5/boot-plugin` and `grails-data-hibernate7/boot-plugin`, the `META-INF/services/org.grails.cli.compiler.CompilerAutoConfiguration` file of each and the `compileOnly` and `testImplementation project(':grails-shell-cli')` lines of the two builds are gone; the class had no spec. The Spring Boot CLI port of the shell is not re-homed, and `grails run <file>.groovy` outside a project has no replacement. `grails-data-hibernate7/README.md`, note 38 of `upgrading90x.adoc` and the upgrade skill say so. Not done: the port itself (`org/grails/cli/{archive,boot,command,compiler,groovy,util}` of `grails-shell-cli`) is deleted with the shell in slice c.
  Implemented, slice d2(iii) of step 5: `grails-datamapping-core` has no dependency on `grails-shell-cli`. The script itself had lost its import of the shell in slice 7 of step 4; what was left was `QueryTailCommandScriptSpec`, which compiled the script with `GroovyScriptCommandFactory` and ran it in an `ExecutionContextImpl` of `GrailsCli`, and the `testCliImplementation(project(':grails-shell-cli'))` line for it. The spec now runs the script on the script host only (`ScriptCommandRunner` with the class path of the test, where the script is found as `META-INF/commands/query-tail.groovy`, as a plugin has it): a capture read once with its summary, no capture, a relative capture file read against the directory of the application and not found from another directory, and a `--top` that is no number, which the script answers with its own message and `false`. Dropped, because it was of the shell alone: the feature that held the `description` of the script (its text, its `usage` and the names of its seven flags), which only the shell evaluates; the host does not call the closure and lists no description. Not done: the script still asks `metaClass.respondsTo(this, 'addCancelledListener')` and registers with the shell where the shell runs it, which is dead once the shell is deleted (slice c) and harmless before.
  Implemented, slice d2(iv) of step 5: the decision is to keep the scripts and take the completer out. The seven scripts of `grails-scaffolding/src/main/scripts` that named `org.grails.cli.interactive.completers.DomainClassCompleter` in their `description` (`create-scaffold-controller`, `create-scaffold-service`, `generate-scaffold-all`, `generate-all`, `generate-controller`, `generate-async-controller`, `generate-views`) have no `completer` line; `install-templates` never had one. Nothing under `grails-scaffolding` names `org.grails.cli`. The scripts already ran on the host, which does not call a `description` closure, so nothing changes there; in the shell, while it is still built, the argument of these seven commands is no longer completed with the names of the domain classes. The scripts are not deleted because the compiled commands of the same names (`src/cli`, `runCommand`) do not do what they do, script by script: `create-scaffold-controller`, `create-scaffold-service` and `generate-scaffold-all` as scripts take a name (`model(args[0])`, in the default package, with no domain class needed) where the commands refuse a name that has no domain class source; `generate-controller`, and `generate-all` through it, writes the service spec to `src/integration-test/groovy` as a script and to `src/test/groovy` as a command; `generate-async-controller` and `generate-views` render the same files and differ in the result only (a script goes on after a domain class that is not found and succeeds, a command fails); `install-templates` is the same copy. `ScriptCommandRunnerSpec` of `grails-core` runs all eight scripts from these sources as before. Not done: the two implementations are not made one, and which of them stays is not decided.

- **e. In-repo consumers leave the profile modules** — `grails-test-examples/**/build.gradle` (the seven profile dependency lines, and the three `index.gsp` copies pointed at `grails-forge/grails-forge-core/src/main/resources/gsp/index.gsp`), `grails-spring-security/{acl,cas}/plugin/build.gradle`, `grails-spring-security/rest/generate-test-apps.sh`, and what `testapp-profile` generated made a pack or a fixture of the test applications. Depends on nothing; can be done beside d1 and d2. Does not touch `settings.gradle` or a `grails.profile` key. Verified by the builds of the examples it changed.
  Implemented, slice e of step 5: no build outside `grails-profiles`, `grails-shell-cli` and `grails-spring-security/rest/testapp-profile` declares a `profile` dependency or reads a file of a profile module. The seven `profile 'org.apache.grails.profiles:...'` lines of `grails-test-examples/{issue-11102,issue-views-182,scaffolding}` and `grails-test-examples/graphql/{grails-docs-app,grails-multi-datastore-app,grails-tenant-app,grails-test-app}` and the `profile project(':grails-profiles-web-plugin')` lines of `grails-spring-security/{acl,cas}/plugin` are gone. `grails-test-examples/{gsp-compile-static,welcome-page,welcome-page-jetty}` stage `grails-forge/grails-forge-core/src/main/resources/META-INF/grails-forge-bundled/gsp/templates/index.gsp`, which the `gsp` feature copies as it is (`template: false`), so the page they compile and serve is the page of a generated application; it has no "Profile" row. `grails-spring-security/rest/generate-test-apps.sh` and `test-app.sh`, which called it, are deleted and nothing was made a pack or a fixture: the scripts could not run in this repository (they read `../gradle.properties` and `spring-security-rest-testapp-profile/features`, neither of which is there, and `test-app.sh` builds with a `gradlew` that is not there either), no build, workflow or test application uses what they generated, and no test example depends on the REST plugin's generated applications. Since then the specs are test examples that the build runs: ten applications under `grails-test-examples/spring-security/rest` (`jwt`, `jwt-bearer`, and `gorm`, `grailscache`, `memcached` and `redis` each in two forms, the second named `-minimal`, with the specs they share in the test fixtures of `common`), written out and not generated. Not done: the `profile` configuration of the nine builds still resolves the default dependency the Grails Gradle plugin gives it until slice b; `grails-spring-security/rest/testapp-profile` (the base skeleton and ten features with the functional specs of the REST plugin, which no build runs), `docker-compose.yml` and `renameFiles.groovy` beside it stay for slice c to delete or to turn into test applications; `FormerCommandsSpec` of `grails-forge-cli` and the `test.inputs` line of its build still read `grails-profiles/*/commands`.
  Implemented after slice e, for slice c: `FormerCommandsSpec` reads no source of the shell or of a profile. What it read (the names of the ten command classes the shell registers, the three words of its interactive mode, and for each profile the files of `commands` with the `synonyms` of the scripts) is the test resource `former-commands-grails-8.tsv` of `grails-forge-cli`, 69 lines of a name and where the command was, written once from the sources at `317898d65d` and not regenerated; its header says so. The spec holds the record whole (69 lines, each well formed, none twice, the thirteen names of the shell, 48 names of profiles) and, as before, that every name is a command somewhere or has a line in `FormerCommands`, so it fails when the table loses a line. The two `test.inputs` declarations of the build for `grails-profiles` and `grails-shell-cli` are gone; the one for the guide page stays.

- **c. Root build** — the one slice that changes `settings.gradle` (root, and the `DirectoryScanner` workaround there and in `end-to-end`, `build-logic`, `grails-gradle` and `grails-forge`, after checking what else packages a `.gitignore`), `gradle/publish-root-config.gradle`, `gradle/rat-root-config.gradle`, `build.gradle` (`profileProjects`), `build-logic` (`PublishPlugin`: the `grails-profile` plugin id and the `profile.yml` sidecar, with their specs), the five `grails-bom/*/build.gradle` (`forcedGroupPrefixes`, the `grails-publish-profile` test in `base`), `dependencies.gradle` and `gradle.properties` (`spring-boot-cli`, the Maven resolver, Tooling API, jopt-simple, expectit and jnr-posix versions, which only the shell uses), `.github/dependency-graph/external-references.yml`, `etc/bin/verify-jar-artifacts.sh`, `etc/bin/reset-verify.sh`, `THREAT_MODEL.md` and `threat-model.yaml`. It takes `grails-shell-cli`, the seven `grails-profiles-*` projects and `grails-spring-security-rest-testapp-profile` out of the build and deletes their directories, in two commits (the shell, then the profiles). Depends on d1, d2 and e: a `project(':grails-shell-cli')` or a file of `grails-profiles` that is still used fails the build. Verified by `./gradlew build -PskipTests`, `validateDependencyVersions`, `buildHealth`, RAT, the build of `grails-forge` and of `end-to-end`, and the release artifact verification scripts. `RENAME.md` and `etc/bin/rename_gradle_artifacts.sh` describe the move of old coordinates and stay.
  Implemented, slice c of step 5: the root build has no Grails shell and no profile module. `grails-shell-cli` (the port of the Spring Boot CLI with it), the seven `grails-profiles-*` projects and `grails-spring-security-rest-testapp-profile` are out of `settings.gradle` and their directories are deleted, with `docker-compose.yml` and `renameFiles.groovy` of `grails-spring-security/rest`, which only the applications generated from that profile used (a memcached and a Redis server for their specs, and a rename of generated files). The functional specs of the Spring Security REST plugin were in `testapp-profile`, which no build ran; they are deleted and were not made test examples, and the commit before this slice is the last that has them. They were made test examples afterwards: six applications under `grails-test-examples/spring-security/rest` (`jwt`, `jwt-bearer`, `gorm`, `grailscache`, `memcached`, `redis`, with the specs they share in the test fixtures of `common`), whose `integrationTest` tasks the Spring Security job of the workflow runs, with an Ersatz server as the OAuth provider and containers for memcached and Redis. Gone with the projects: their entries in `gradle/publish-root-config.gradle` and the `grails-publish-profile` branch there, the RAT exclusions of the two trees and of the `bin` directory of the shell's scripts, `profileProjects` of `build.gradle`, the `grails-profile` plugin id and the `profile.yml` sidecar in `PublishPlugin` of `build-logic` with the part of `PublishPluginSpec` for it, the forced property prefix for `org.apache.grails.profiles` in the five BOM builds and the test for the `grails-publish-profile` plugin in `grails-bom/base` (the mechanism in `PropertyNameCalculator` and its spec stay), and the seven `grails-profiles-*` purls of `.github/dependency-graph/external-references.yml`, which named coordinates that were never published. Version keys: `apacheMavenVersion`, `apacheMavenResolverVersion`, `gradleToolingApiVersion`, `joptSimpleVersion`, `expectitCoreVersion` and `jnrPosixVersion` of `gradle.properties`, and the `spring-boot-cli` entry of `gradleBomDependencies`, so the Grails BOMs no longer manage `org.springframework.boot:spring-boot-cli`; nothing else in the five builds used one of them. Kept: `spring-retry.version`, for applications that use `@Retryable` (its comment no longer names the shell); `maven-model.version`, `plexus-xml.version`, `jline.version` and `ant.version`, which `build-logic`, `grails-gradle` and other modules use; and the `grails-shell-cli` purl of the dependency graph file, which lists what earlier releases published, as it does `org.grails/grails-shell`. `verify-jar-artifacts.sh` looks for `grailsw`, `grailsw.bat` and `grails-wrapper.jar` under `grails-wrapper/` of the `grails-forge-core` jar, which is where a generated application gets them from, in place of the skeleton of the base profile jar, and `reset-verify.sh` removes the directory of that check. `THREAT_MODEL.md` and `threat-model.yaml` describe the command line of `grails-forge` and feature packs taken by their coordinates (`PackArtifacts`) where they had the shell and `MavenProfileRepository`. `query-tail.groovy` has no `addCancelledListener` branch: the shutdown hook is what stops it. `ScriptNameResolverTests` of `grails-test-suite-uber`, which tests a class of `grails-bootstrap` and only sat in the package `org.grails.cli`, is in `org.grails.build.parsing`. The `grails-cli.yml` of the test examples `geb` and `geb-context-path`, which the command line reads as the earlier record of a project, names `grails-project-settings` where it named `grails-profiles`. Not removed: the lines of the five settings scripts (root, `build-logic`, `grails-gradle`, `grails-forge`, `end-to-end`) that take `.gitignore` and `.gitattributes` out of Ant's default excludes. No source set or copy of any build has such a file now (Forge writes those of an application from templates of other names), but `new PatternSet()` of Gradle uses a static `PatternSpecFactory` that keeps the default excludes of the first build of a daemon, so in a daemon that ran a build with those lines the `Sync` tasks of a build without them fail with "Cannot change default excludes during the build" (seen for `agentSkillResources`; the same tasks pass in a new JVM). The release branches that have profiles share daemons with this one, so the lines stay until none of them is built, and their comments say so. Not done: the comments of the wrapper properties of `grails-gradle` and `grails-forge` still name `gradleToolingApiVersion`; the exclusion of the vulnerability scan for `android-json` still says it comes through `spring-boot-cli`, which was not checked against a scan; `upgrading90x.adoc` and the upgrade skill have no note that the shell and the profiles are not published and that the BOMs no longer manage them or `spring-boot-cli`; the `profile` configuration of every Grails project still has its default dependency on a profile, which no build publishes any more, and the Gradle skill still lists the two profile plugin ids (slice b).
- **b. Gradle plugins** — `grails-gradle/**`: the `grails-profile` and `grails-publish-profile` plugins (`plugins/build.gradle`, `plugin/profiles/**`, `ProfileCompilerTaskSpec`), `configureProfile` and `getDefaultProfile` in `GrailsGradlePlugin` and `GrailsPluginGradlePlugin` with the rejection diagnostic for a `profile` declaration that takes their place, `GrailsClasspathToolingModelBuilder` and `GrailsClasspath.getProfileDependencies`, `BuildSettings.PROFILE` and `PROFILE_REPOSITORIES`, `org.grails.cli.profile.*` of the model with `GroovyScriptCommandTransform` and the `jline` line of `model/build.gradle`, `BomPlatformFunctionalSpec` with its fixture, `plugins/README.md`. Depends on c, which removes the projects that apply the plugins and the shell that compiles against the model, and on e for the builds that declare `profile`. Verified by the `grails-gradle` build and by a TestKit spec of the diagnostic, for a declaration in a build script and for one made through `dependencies.add`.
  Implemented, slice b of step 5: the Grails Gradle plugins have nothing of the profiles or of the shell. Removed: the plugins `org.apache.grails.gradle.grails-profile` and `grails-publish-profile` (`GrailsProfileGradlePlugin`, `GrailsProfilePublishGradlePlugin`, `ProfileCompilerTask`, their registrations and `ProfileCompilerTaskSpec`); `configureProfile` and `getDefaultProfile` of `GrailsGradlePlugin` and `GrailsPluginGradlePlugin`, with the default dependency on `org.apache.grails.profiles:<web|web-plugin>`, the resolution rule that gave a profile the version of Grails and the read of the `grails.profile` system property; `BuildSettings.PROFILE` and `PROFILE_REPOSITORIES`, removed and not deprecated, because `BuildSettings` is in `grails-gradle/model` and no build of the repository names either constant; the tooling model as a whole (`GrailsClasspath`, `DefaultGrailsClasspath`, `GrailsClasspathToolingModelBuilder`, `registerToolingModelBuilder`), because nothing in the five builds asks for it now that the shell is gone, and the IntelliJ plugin has a model builder of its own ("IDE integration" in 2.5); with it the constructors of `GrailsGradlePlugin`, `GrailsWebGradlePlugin` and `GrailsPluginGradlePlugin`, which took a `ToolingModelBuilderRegistry` for nothing else; and `org.grails.cli.profile.CommandDescription`, `CommandArgument` and `GroovyScriptCommandTransform` of the model with its `compileOnly` dependency on JLine, which only `ProfileCompilerTask` and the shell used. Kept in the model, because they are what the script host and the catalog compile against: `grails.util.BuildSettings`, `Environment` and `GrailsNameUtils`, `org.grails.build.parsing.*`, `org.grails.io.support.*` (`ScriptCommandRunner` and the classes beside it in `grails-core/src/cli`), and `org.apache.grails.gradle.model.cli.*` (`CommandCatalogBuilder`, which uses `GrailsNameUtils` and `FactoriesLoaderSupport`). The rejection diagnostic: the plugins register a configuration named `profile` (`GrailsGradlePlugin.REMOVED_PROFILE_CONFIGURATION`) that cannot be resolved or consumed and whose dependency set has a listener (`whenObjectAdded`) that throws. A declaration in the `dependencies` block, one through `dependencies.add` and one through `dependencies.addProvider` all fail while the build is configured, at the line of the build script, with `Grails 9 has no profiles: remove the 'profile' dependency '<group:name[:version]>' from the build of <root project 'name' | project ':path'>. Nothing reads it, and the profiles are not published for Grails 9. See "The Grails Shell and the Profiles Are Not Published, and a Build Cannot Declare a Profile" in the Grails 9 upgrade notes: https://grails.apache.org/docs/latest/guide/upgrading.html`. A listener and not a check after evaluation, so nothing is deferred and the line is named; a set that is listened to realizes what a provider adds, which is why that form is caught too. The configuration is registered lazily, holds nothing, is never resolved, and is left out of the configurations the BOM platform is contributed to, since the platform would be refused as a profile; a failed configuration stores no configuration cache entry, and a build without the line stores and reuses one as before. A `profile` configuration that the build made before the plugin was applied is left as it is. Tests: `RemovedProfileConfigurationSpec` (TestKit; the three forms for an application and the first two for a plugin project, a build without the line built twice with the configuration cache for both, and the configuration of the build's own) and `BomPlatformFunctionalSpec`, which held that the configuration kept its default profile and now holds that it is given nothing. With it: upgrade note 39 and its entry in the upgrade skill, note 35 corrected (the line has to be removed, not only left out), the two Gradle pages of the guide and the row of "What Became of Profiles", the two plugin ids of the Gradle skill, `plugins/README.md`, and the comments of the wrapper properties of `grails-gradle` and `grails-forge`, so what the paragraph of slice c lists as not done for slice b is done. Not done: `.sdkmanrc` and the comment of `gradleWrapperProperties.gsp` of Forge still name `gradleToolingApiVersion`; a dependency constraint declared for `profile` is not refused, since it adds no dependency; the message links the guide of the latest release, not that of the version of the build.
- **g. The profile at run time** — `grails-core` (`GrailsApplicationPostProcessor`, `Settings.PROFILE`, and the decision on `profiles` of a plugin: `GrailsPlugin.PROFILES`, `DefaultGrailsPlugin.isEnabled(String[])`, the sixteen plugin classes), Forge (`GrailsProjectSettings` stops writing the key; the row of `gsp/index.gsp` and `welcome.app.profile` in the nineteen message bundles; `def profiles` in `templates/plugin.gsp`; `grails-forge-web/grails-app/conf/application.yml`), and the keys in the configuration files of the repository. Depends on a only; shares no file with b, c, d or e and can be done beside them. It is the slice the design gates on migration fixtures: `@Profile`, profile-specific configuration, an explicit `spring.profiles.active`, and a plugin that declares `profiles`, each with and without the key. Verified by those fixtures in `grails-core`, the Forge suites and the functional tests of the REST API examples.
- **f. Documentation** — `grails-doc/**`, the one slice that changes `toc.yml`: the profiles chapter replaced by the feature packs chapter, `REST/restProfile.adoc`, the command-line and getting-started pages, the command reference (`create-profile`, `list-profiles` and `profile-info` deleted), `GRAILS_PREFERRED_SHELL` and `-t` taken out. It follows d1 for the pages about the launcher and c for the pages about profiles; the pages that only describe feature packs can be written at any time. Verified by the guide build.
  Implemented, slice f of step 5: the guide describes one command line. The profiles chapter (`profiles.adoc` and the thirteen pages under `profiles/`) is deleted and the chapter "Application Types, Features and Feature Packs" (`applicationTypes.adoc`, key `applicationTypes` in `toc.yml`) stands in its place, with three pages: the features of an application, "Customizing What a New Application Gets", and "What Became of Profiles", a table of each thing a profile had with what there is now and links to notes 35 to 37 of `upgrading90x.adoc`. `plugins/forgeFeaturePacks.adoc` is not moved: `PackTemplateApiSpec` of `grails-forge-core` reads it at that path and every `xref:plugins#forgeFeaturePacks` stays as it is; the new chapter links to it. `REST/restProfile.adoc` is `REST/restApiApplication.adoc`, "The REST API Application Type" (anchor `restApiApplication`). The introduction of `commandLine.adoc`, which described the lookup of a command in a profile, the two interactive mode pages, `downloadingAndInstalling.adoc` (three commands, `-t`, the shell and its profiles) and the other getting-started pages, the plugin pages, `contributing/build.adoc` (`:grails-cli:installDist` of the `grails-forge` build) and every example with `-t forge` are the Grails 9 command line. `creatingProject.adoc` no longer documents `-t`/`--test` and `--jdk=11`, which the create commands do not have, and has `-r`/`--reloading`, `neo4j` for `--data` and the JDK values. The environment in front of a command (`grails test war`), which the command line refuses, is `-Dgrails.env` after it in `conf/environments.adoc`, `deployingAnApplication.adoc`, the pages on running tests and the reference; `query-tail` is shown as `gradle runScriptCommand --arg=query-tail`, which is what the command line names for it. In the command reference the pages `create-profile`, `list-profiles`, `profile-info`, `list-plugins`, `plugin-info` and `create-hibernate-cfg-xml` (a command of Grails 2 that nothing has) are deleted; 33 pages are written again for the one command line from `help` of each command (the part about the shell and the "Grails Forge CLI" part of a page are one text); `feature-add`, `project-show`, `project-regenerate` and `gradle` are new. The Grails 9 section of `whatsNew.adoc` has an entry for the one command line. In `upgrading90x.adoc`, note 34 tells a plugin to remove the completer line, the notes refer to each other by number, and note 37 has the `--profile` option and a profile of one's own. Not done: `gradleBuild/gradlePlugins.adoc` still lists the `grails-profile` and `grails-publish-profile` Gradle plugins and `gradleDependencies.adoc` names the `profile` configuration, which exist until slice b; the guide still calls the command line "the Grails Forge CLI" in the pages earlier slices wrote; the reference pages `generate-all`, `generate-controller` and `generate-views` keep their text of the shell beside that of `runCommand`; `conf/dataSource/queryTail.adoc` and the `query-tail` reference page still say that the command runs outside Gradle and reads `JAVA_OPTS`, which was true of the shell and was not checked for `runScriptCommand`; the screenshots of the interactive mode of the shell (`interactive-*.png`) are in the image directories and no page shows them; `index.adoc` and `reference.adoc`, which the guide build does not read, were changed only for the pages that were removed and added; presets are not described, there being none.

Two files are written by every slice and by none alone: `upgrading90x.adoc`, where each slice adds its own section at the end and the number is given when it is merged, and this document, where each slice writes under its own entry.

Order: a; then d1, the four parts of d2, e and g side by side (seven people at most); then c; then b; f beside them as said. c and b are one person each and are not done beside each other.

### Step 6 — documentation, website and hosted rollout (9.0.x; alongside each step)

Replace the 9.0 profiles chapter with "Feature packs and presets", rewrite command-line and command-reference pages, and put all additions/removals/semantic changes in `upgrading90x`. Document committed baseline storage, migration limits, trusted template execution, offline behavior and the descriptor/command APIs. Update plugin-authoring and metadata-repository guides, website index references, hosted preview/diff handling and the official-feature UI. No 8.1 pack instructions or `upgrading81x` unification additions are introduced.

**Exit:** the 9.0 guide and executable examples build; official hosted generation matches the CLI's locked generation state; the 8.1 guide and hosted version slots continue to describe/support profiles. Documentation ships with each user-facing implementation step rather than waiting until the end.

Deferred: pack-scaffolding convenience commands, classifier artifacts, additional template engines, automatic feature removal and generator/pack upgrades. The supported deprecated script DSL remains throughout 9.x and is eligible for removal in the next major. Size work after the selection, replay and command-API prototypes; steps 2–5 span multiple builds/repositories and are not "Forge-only" tasks.

<a id="decisions"></a>
## 6. Decisions and implementation gates

The release boundary is a confirmed maintainer decision. The other entries state the working design adopted in this revision; their proof obligations are explicit, not claims that the implementation already exists.

| # | Topic | Decision / gate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Template language | GSP source for external packs; matching precompiled official packs. Versioned public model API and declared/tested generator compatibility; no logic-less fallback in v1. |
| 2 | Pack publication | Main JAR only under `META-INF/grails-forge/`; validate/check-compile in isolated Gradle tool workers. Classifier support is deferred and would need separate artifact checksums. |
| 3 | Catalog | Extend `apache/grails-plugins-metadata`; CLI-only external discovery, release-pinned offline copy, immutable locked resolutions. Ordinary plugin and usable-pack searches have different filters. |
| 4 | Project state | Commit manifest, exact lock and rendered baseline; ignore only derived caches/run/history/transaction state. Accept duplicated generated content in exchange for a portable merge ancestor. |
| 5 | Scripts | Keep the documented profile-free DSL deprecated through 9.x; convert inventoried commands and document direct-profile-API migration before removal. |
| 6 | Release lines | **Confirmed:** 8.1 contains the Grails-app conversion and retains profiles; all unification additions, cutover and removals target 9.0.x. |
| 7 | Hosted Forge | Official bundled features only. No external catalog fetch, pack execution, private repositories or `feature add` in the hosted API. |
| 8 | Fetcher | Exact-artifact transport, scoped repositories, complete-artifact hashing, explicit local test exceptions and documented settings.xml subset; no Maven runtime dependency resolver. |
| 9 | Runtime Spring profiles | Remove `grails.profile` injection on 9.0; test and document deliberate migration of conditional runtime behavior, including the implicit `web` profile. |
| 10 | Presets | One preset, no inheritance in v1; same compatibility/contribution model, explicit precedence and named unsupported custom-profile cases. Delivery gate before profile removal. |
| 11 | CLI/IDE API | `org.apache.grails:grails-cli` and new `org.apache.grails.cli.GrailsCli`; schema-versioned JSON catalog with completeness/diagnostics. Test the IDE against both old 8.1 and new 9.0 contracts. |
| 12 | Build fragments | Typed contribution channels first; deterministic escape-hatch fragments at defined anchors. No arbitrary build-script semantic validation or guaranteed conflict-free merge is promised. |
| 13 | Feature selection | Canonical feature/group IDs, explicit choice precedence, requirement cycles accepted as mutual requirements, and deterministic closure shared by compiled and declarative features. |
| 14 | Feature application | Verify replay against the committed ancestor, true three-way merge, whole-operation conflict detection and journaled recovery. Update baseline to pristine proposed output, not user edits. |
| 15 | Migration ancestry | Existing marker/build evidence creates an untracked manifest unless an exact ancestor is known. Explicit reviewed adoption establishes a new baseline; scanning Gradle cannot reconstruct one. |
| 16 | Command execution | Explicit targets instead of `requiresApplicationContext`/`requiresConsole` combinations; context-bound compatibility default, scoped service capabilities and tested classloader/process cleanup. |

Implementation spikes must settle the exact JSON/YAML schemas and supported version-comparison rules, prove source compilation/caching, demonstrate text/binary transaction recovery on supported OSes, and verify the proposed launcher publication and precompiled-companion compatibility. Record any resulting contract changes here before publishing the APIs. These gates do not reopen the 9.0 release assignment.

<a id="testing"></a>
## 7. Testing and documentation

Tests exercise public generator/CLI/build contracts, not private implementation helpers. In addition to the repository's affected-module checks, the implementation needs:

- **Selection/schema:** unknown keys, namespaced IDs, ambiguous aliases, duplicate versions, cycles/diamonds, default/explicit/required/excluded precedence, mixed compiled/declarative groups, final predicates and deterministic order. Validate the descriptor examples against the implemented schema.
- **Templates/contributions:** supported/unsupported template APIs and generator ranges, first-use/cache replay, modified local sources, deterministic bytes/modes, typed/static model failures, GSP rendering failures, dependency versions/exclusions/kind conflicts, configuration shape conflicts and normalized path collisions. Golden tests cover each converted official feature; generated Gradle builds cover free-form fragments.
- **Transport/catalog:** main-JAR identity and digest, private/exclusive repository routing, scoped credentials/redirects, malformed metadata, timestamped snapshots, offline success/failure, refresh without unlock, failed/partial downloads, concurrent cache access and zip/local-path guards. Use local synthetic repositories/catalogs rather than depending on the live public index.
- **Gradle authoring:** isolated compiler/generator classpaths, source-only packaging, preset-only publication, `forgePackTest`, supported application-type/option fixtures and configuration-cache reuse. Publish the fixtures through `build/local-maven` using the repository's normal test-publication chain.
- **Project state/transactions:** fresh-clone replay, all options including non-default GORM/servlet, missing/changed inputs, user text edits and deletions, binary files, executable bits, CRLF/LF, new-file collisions, concurrent edits, failed writes and interrupted recovery. Assert that conflicts preserve every existing project/state file and that repeat application is a no-op. Rendered baseline state must not recursively include itself.
- **Migration:** 8.0/8.1 Forge and built-in-profile fixtures, absent/legacy markers, custom preset mapping, dynamic/indirect Gradle declarations, multi-project roots and unsupported skeletons. Cover untracked/adopted state and active-profile behavior with conditional beans and configuration. Migration preflight works when Gradle configuration is broken.
- **Commands:** reserved/duplicate names, aliases/abbreviations, legacy `runCommand` routing, app-local discovery, supported precompiled companions, stale/incomplete catalogs, clean JSON stdout, lazy help, non-interactive exits, classloader refresh and cancellation/process ownership. Test every retained/changed/retired command in the step-4 matrix, including REST scaffolding tests and plugin publication adapters.
- **End-to-end/distribution:** generate → edit → feature add → build/run; migrate an 8.1 project to 9.0; script-DSL compatibility; CLI wrapper and SDKMAN artifact contents; IDE 8.1/9.0 runs and lifecycle state; Windows/macOS/Linux paths and permissions. Hosted tests prove that external packs are never resolved and that ZIP/preview/diff metadata handling is consistent.
- **Documentation:** descriptor/template/command API references, authoring fixture, migration/adoption guide, trusted execution and offline semantics, complete command reference and `upgrading90x` additions/removals. The 8.1 guide continues to document profiles and its existing CLI behavior.

<a id="appendix-a"></a>
## Appendix A: command tree

```
grails
  [--plain-output] [--non-interactive] [--offline] [--refresh]
  create-app | create-restapi | create-plugin | create-web-plugin | create-rest-api-plugin
      [--features a,b,g:a:v] [--exclude-features x] [--preset g:a:v]
      [--data|--servlet|--jdk|--reloading] [--inplace] [--subproject] [--list-features] [--feature-pack-dir <path>]
  feature   list | search <text> | info <id> | diff <id|gav> | add <id|gav> [--dry-run|--diff]
  plugin    search <text> | info <name|coordinates>
  project   show [--json] | regenerate (--output <dir> [--force] | --diff) | migrate [NAME] [--type …] [--features …] [--exclude-features …] [--jdk|--servlet|--data|--reloading] [--dry-run] [--force] | init [--dry-run] (not built)
  create-controller | create-service | create-domain-class | create-taglib | create-interceptor
  create-unit-test | create-integration-test | create-functional-test | create-restful-controller
  create-domain-resource | create-command | create-script | create-job | add-property | <pack artefacts>
  run-app | stop-app | test-app | compile | clean | assemble | war | package | console | shell
  dependency-report | run-command | run-script | stats | bug-report | install-templates
  publish-plugin | package-plugin
  gradle <args> | open <path> | help [command] [--json]
  <discovered application/standalone/console commands: dbm-*, schema-export, generate-*, s2-*, ...>
```

Commands are available only when their application type or selected feature supplies them. Offline mode controls CLI transport; an explicitly invoked Gradle task needs its corresponding offline argument forwarded too and cannot be promised network-free if its own code makes network calls. `--refresh` never changes locked pack versions. `project init` is baseline adoption, not a second application generator, and is not built; `project migrate` writes an untracked record from what the project shows. Authoring scaffolding, automatic removal and upgrade commands are not part of this v1 tree.

<a id="appendix-b"></a>
## Appendix B: facts the plan relies on

The first group was checked against the local code baseline for revision 11. External repository counts/paths and the SQL-tail branch observations retain the original inventory date and must be refreshed by the corresponding implementation work.

- Forge has no declarative mechanism; features are `@Component`s found by scanning `org.grails.forge` (`ForgeCoreConfiguration.groovy:35-50`), registered by `FeatureRegistry` (`feature/FeatureRegistry.groovy:43-64`).
- The build file snapshots dependencies at `FeaturePhase.BUILD` (`GradleBuildCreator.groovy:34-74`); later additions are lost.
- `Dependency` had no exclusions before step 1 added `Dependency.Builder.exclude`; equality compares group/artifact/scope but not version, platform kind or exclusions. Since step 1 a second declaration with different exclusions is an error; a second declaration with a different version or platform kind is still dropped silently, and no error names the contributing features yet. `Builder.extension`, which `buildInternal` dropped, is removed. Versions come from bundled BOM metadata, literals or POM lookup.
- `FeatureContext.isPresent` is class-based and `addFeature` immediately recurses (`FeatureContext.groovy:110-127`); `OneOfFeatureValidator` validates by group ID since step 1. Neither provides a descriptor-ID dependency solver. `ContextFactory` already computes compiled defaults to a fixed point; the new resolver must preserve that intent while incorporating requirements/exclusions.
- Codegen commands are a static list (`ForgeCli.groovy:45-57`) gated only by a marker file in the current directory (`:115-128`).
- `FeatureDiffer` is exposed through web `DiffController`; its baseline uses an empty feature selection and an `Options` constructor that restores default GORM/servlet (`FeatureDiffer.groovy:89-100`). `MapOutputHandler.write` stores null for binary outputs (`:40-47`), which the differ skips. This cannot supply the proposed byte-preserving historical merge ancestor unchanged.
- Inside a project the shell dispatches everything through the profile (`GrailsCli.groovy:385`); the profile jars come from the Gradle `profile` configuration via the `GrailsClasspath` tooling model (`GrailsClasspathToolingModelBuilder.groovy:43-60`); Aether is used only outside a project.
- The proposed committed `.grails/` generation-state contract does not exist in the reviewed generator; existing lifecycle PID state is under `build/`.
- `grails.codegen.defaultPackage` has runtime readers; `grails.profile` becomes an active Spring profile with a `web` default.
- Gradle discovers `-cli` names from manifests/factories without loading implementation classes (`GrailsCliGradlePlugin.groovy:451-479`). The runtime `ApplicationContextCommandRegistry` does load and instantiate commands (`:53-90`); metadata-only catalog discovery is new work. `ApplicationCommand` currently requires the context-oriented `handle(ExecutionContext)` contract, with no standalone/console flags.
- The IntelliJ plugin (`apache/grails-intellij-plugin`, `main`, 2026-10-11) starts its own JVM with main class `org.grails.cli.GrailsCli` on the resolved `grails-shell-cli` artifact (`Grails3GradleCommandExecutor.java:54`, `GrailsModuleModelBuilderImpl.java:54-78`), hard-codes 34 shell command names (`Grails3CommandProvider.java:31-70`), runs `run-app` the same way (`GrailsRunConfiguration.java:70`), detects projects by `grails-app` (`GrailsApplicationManager.java:167`), and creates projects through the hosted Forge API (`GrailsForgeModuleBuilder.kt:67-221`).
- `sql-tail` (branch `feat/sql-tail-command`, PR #16631 on 9.0.x) is a `GroovyScriptCommand` script in `grails-datamapping-core/src/cli/resources/META-INF/commands/sql-tail.groovy` that follows a capture file written by the running application and never boots it; `run-app --sql-tail` is a flag added to `grails-profiles/base/commands/run-app.groovy`. `DbmGormDiffCommand` is an `ApplicationCommand` that needs the booted context (`withGormDatabase(applicationContext, dataSource)`).
- The plugin portal's data source is `apache/grails-plugins-metadata`: one YAML per plugin under `grails-plugins/<group path>/<artifactId>.yml` (`name`, `desc`, `coords`, `owner`, `vcs`, `docs`, `maven-repo`, `labels`, `licenses`, `versions`, optional `deprecated`), `index-plugins.groovy` run every eight hours by `.github/workflows/updateIndex.yml`, which downloads each new version's jar by exact URL and reads `META-INF/grails-plugin.xml`, and `grails-plugins-index.json` consumed by `apache/grails-static-website` (`buildSrc/.../PluginsTask.groovy:67`). 256 entries on 2026-10-13.

## Change log

Earlier entries describe earlier proposals, including release assignments superseded by revision 11.

| Revision | Date | Change |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 52 | 2026-11-22 | Forge's own environment. The command line, `PackCheck` and `PackFixtures` set production whatever environment their JVM is given, as `PackPagesCompiler` already did; before, a `grails.env` or `GRAILS_ENV` meant for a project switched the command line into development (watching the working directory) and made the templates compile without the pages of the build. One class, `ForgeEnvironment`, says why (section 2.2, "Template language and compatibility"). Decided by the maintainer: Forge is a production application that generates projects for development, GSP is how it renders templates and nothing is reloaded, and the environment is not the user's to set. |
| 51 | 2026-11-23 | Integrated the completed prebuilt-framework and pack-reproducibility work with current 9.0.x. `-PprebuiltTestRepository` omits framework and Gradle-plugin composite substitution, validates publication completion, version, Java target, indy/classic variant and canonical framework source fingerprints, and supplies the BOM and wrapper outputs that Forge needs. CI shares baseline test repositories; no-tests builds keep their smaller composite dependency graph, and Forge coverage reuses the framework built for core coverage. Locale-neutral generation and help, declaration-order ties, canonical imports, total catalog version ordering and both timestamp-free shadow stages have public regression guards and documented reproducibility limits. Preserved the packaged compiled-page fix and upstream release-JDK, documentation, scaffolding and security fixes. Reconciled #16968 with the compiled ACL command: its actual templates emit value equality, hash codes and serialization, with command-level emitted-source tests and non-overwrite coverage; the guide keeps the migrated invocation and warns how to update older generated classes. |
| 50 | 2026-11-22 | Speed, decided by the maintainer after the measurements of revision 49. The start scripts (`grails`, `grails.bat`, `grailsw`, `grailsw.bat`) give the JVM `-XX:TieredStopAtLevel=1` when they are started with a command: a command is a short-lived process whose time is JVM start and class loading, and without the optimizing compiler it is 15–20% faster and `create-app` takes a third of the CPU time (9.0 s to 2.7 s). The cost is stated at the flag: long-running hot code is significantly slower without C2. That is accepted because builds, tests and the application run in the JVMs of Gradle and of the application, which do not get the flag, and interactive mode, the one long-lived JVM of the command line, is started without an argument and is not given it either, since a repeated command there was measured slower with it (`stats` 44 to 78 ms). `GRAILS_OPTS=-XX:TieredStopAtLevel=4` restores the default. The command line also loads the classes it is about to use on a second thread (`StartupPreload`): the classes of a checked-in list without initializing them, and Groovy's `FormatHelper`, whose first use scans the JDK image (the same in Groovy 4 and 5; its cause is GROOVY-9646, open); off with `-Dgrails.cli.preload=false` and where the JVM has one processor. Together: `--version` 523 to 365 ms, `create-controller` 675 to 476 ms, `create-app` 1961 to 1361 ms, the same through the Grails wrapper (`--version` 489 to 320 ms). The templates of the packs of Forge are compiled when Forge is built (`:grails-forge-core:compilePackPages`, `PackPagesCompiler`, `PrecompiledPackPages`): the jar has a class `PackPage_<key>` for each of the 29 templates, named by the SHA-256 `PackPages` keeps a page by (feature, directives, text), and an index `META-INF/grails-forge-pages/index`; a run still parses the template and takes the class only when the SHA-256 of the Groovy source the parser gives and of the compiler and its settings is the one of the index, so another text, feature, environment (the build compiles for production, which the command line runs in), Grails or Groovy version, a pack that is not of Forge, and a missing or damaged index or class are compiled when rendered as before; the index and the classes come only from the class loader of Forge; `-Dgrails.forge.pack.precompiled=false` turns it off; measured about 140 ms of wall time and 0.9 s of CPU time less per `create-app`. A provider-like indirection between compiling a template and the environment was considered and is not needed: the templates read only the model, at render time. The publish to the repository of the tests gained an opt-in property, `-PtestRepositoryWithoutSbom=true` (off by default), that builds the jars without their SBOM and so takes the 156 CycloneDX tasks out of the chain (about a tenth of the summed task time; no wall-time gain measurable on a many-core machine); it is opt-in because the SBOM is inside the jar, so the specs would test jars that differ from the released ones, it takes effect only together with the slim publish, any other build ignores it with a message, and a publish to another repository from a slimmed build fails. In CI the Windows cell went from 70–85 to 41 minutes with the slim publish of revision 49. *Not done: a class archive (AppCDS).* Measured before the C1 start and the preload existed (macOS, JDK 21, cold JVM): a dynamic archive made from one `create-app` run (41 MB) took `--version` from 405 to 328 ms, `--help` in a project from 533 to 418 ms and `create-app` from 1524 to 1180 ms, and with C1 to 277, 351 and 995 ms. The variant without a build step, `-XX:+AutoCreateSharedArchive -XX:SharedArchiveFile=…` (JDK 19 and later), cost 110 ms on the first run for a 13.6 MB file and then gave 323 ms for `--version` but only 1463 ms for `create-app`, because the archive holds what the first command loaded and is not extended. What it would need: an archive is valid for one build of one JDK and one jar, so it has to be made on the user's machine, at installation or on first run, in a directory the user can write, and thrown away when either changes; a first run that pays for writing it; and a decision on who cleans up archives of earlier versions. It does not apply to the Grails wrapper, which loads the command line through a class loader of its own. Open: how much is left to gain now that the classes are loaded ahead on a second thread; whether SDKMAN and the zip can run a step at installation; behaviour on Windows and with a read-only installation directory; and whether the AOT cache of JDK 24 and later (JEP 483) is the better thing to wait for. |
| 49 | 2026-11-22 | Windows as run, and speed as measured. After three runs of the Windows cell every specification of the command line passes there. First run: 36 failures, 31 of them the ownership rule and the wrapper update that revision 48 had already changed; the rest were a deleted command that was noticed late (the directory is now asked for its change time, not the listing above it), a drive-less `file:` repository address that was kept while the path it stands for was left out (both are left out; a UNC `file://server/share` stays a directory), bug-report naming project paths with `\\`, and a functional test that needs a container the runner does not have. Second run: the ownership rule, the wrapper's two-run self-update through real batch files and the argument round trip through `cmd.exe` passed on the runner; the only new failures were a specification that looked for the argument refusal among run errors instead of where the command prints it as a usage error (exit 2). Still not shown anywhere: that a detached application survives Ctrl-C and the closing of its console, and the rule for an administrator who is not elevated. Two wrappers that start together no longer fail on making the directory of the command line (`Files.createDirectories` for `File.mkdirs`). Measured where the time of the command line goes (one machine, macOS, JDK 21, the installed distribution): the logic of a command is 15–35 ms, and the rest of the 430–630 ms of a command is the cold start of the JVM — the Groovy runtime about 150 ms, of which 58 ms is one scan Groovy 6 does when a value is first formatted, and the 2,000 classes of the command line 150–260 ms; a command that generates adds about 650 ms for the generator, and `create-app` compiles the templates of the bundled packs each time (about 280 ms). `run-app --detach` now looks for the answer of the application every 100 ms, and `run-app` and `stop-app` for each other every 50 ms, where all looked every 250 ms. Measured and not done: a C1-only start (`-XX:TieredStopAtLevel=1`) takes 15–20% off every command and three quarters of the CPU time of `create-app`, a class archive 20%, both a third; loading classes ahead on a second thread 27–30%; compiling the templates of the bundled packs when Forge is built. Of the specifications, the ones that start real Gradle are 6% of the features and 68% of the time: four of them now run their stub builds in one isolated daemon each, and the publish to `build/local-maven` they depend on leaves out the sources and javadoc jars and the checksums of a release (7 min 15 s to 2 min 55 s from a clean tree; in CI that publish was about 60 of the 85 minutes of the Windows cell). |
| 48 | 2026-11-22 | Windows and coverage. CI gains a Windows cell for the Forge core and command-line specs (`forgeWindows`, which the snapshot publish waits for) and a coverage job for the Forge build; the coverage report of the root build counts the JVMs that specs start (`grails.test.fork.jacoco`, `build/jacoco/<task>-forks`). Decided by the maintainer: the Windows defects are fixed in this pull request. Windows, as implemented after the first CI cell (not yet run there when written): ownership is compared by security identifier, and what `BUILTIN\Administrators` (S-1-5-32-544) owns is the user's own only in an elevated process whose token has the group and whose new files are given to it, with no access control list read; the Grails wrapper completes its own update in at most two runs (the scripts move `grails-wrapper.jar.new` into place before Java starts, a running `grailsw.bat` of Grails 9 is replaced in place because it ends by a label, and one of Grails 7/8 by a stand-in that ends the run at the byte offset `cmd.exe` resumes at); `gradlew.bat` is refused `"%&\|<>^!`, control characters, an unquoted path with `,;=()` and a UNC project directory; a detached build relies on the JDK starting a fully redirected process with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`; stop ends the process tree at once and says that the application was not asked to shut down; file replacement retries for 1.5 s what Windows refuses for a file in use. `DomainFieldModifier` refuses an interface or annotation type, and indents a member that begins a class as its constructors and inner classes are indented. |
| 47 | 2026-11-22 | Decided by the maintainer: `run-app` that does not stay returns when the web server of the application answers `HEAD /` (no `Grails-Startup-Phase`, or `READY`), which can be before `BootStrap` has finished, as before revision 45, which had it wait for `READY`. It waits while the answers of the progress server name another phase, asks the path named in `Grails-Startup-Status-Path` only during that time and only so that a failed start is told to it (a failed start holds the port only for a client that asked that path), ends at once on `FAILED`, and asks no status path of an application without startup progress. The Grails wrapper installs a download only when it is whole: it matches the strongest checksum file the repository has beside the jar (SHA-512, SHA-256, SHA-1, MD5) or, without one, every entry matches its CRC, and the jar has its start class; it writes beside the jar and moves atomically, repairs an installed jar that cannot be started with one download, passes over a repository whose metadata or download is bad with one line and fails only when every repository missed, naming each with its reason and no stack trace, stops at once on a home that cannot be written to, has timeouts, prints no credentials of an address, and no longer has `cleanupOtherVersions`, which never removed an installed version. The threat model says what the checksum is worth: it finds a download that was cut off, and is no protection against a repository that is malicious. Also decided: the wrapper of an earlier version keeps replacing its three committed files (revision 44); `create-web-plugin` keeps the URL mappings of a web application; the order of the test phases, `--clean` as running the tests again, `--debug-jvm` for each phase in turn, and the warning for an environment after the name of a plugin command stay as they are. |
| 46 | 2026-11-22 | Correction of the hybrid of revision 45, and its reasons written down: the new subsection "How Gradle is run: a hybrid, and why" of section 2.5 is the decision as it stands. The default is by where a command is run. A command line that runs one command starts the Gradle wrapper as a process for every command, as before the hybrid (Gradle's own terminal output, inherited input, the messages with the exit code, the client process a foreground `run-app` records): the Tooling API saved such a command less than a tenth of a second and cost it Gradle's output on the terminal. The interactive console (`ConsoleCommands.isInteractive`, set only by `ForgeCli.interactive`; a command line run in a caller's JVM is not the console) runs the builds it asks for through the Tooling API on one kept connection, with plain output, no input and cancellation by token, while `gradle`, `shell` and a `run-app` that leaves the application running start the wrapper there too. `GRAILS_GRADLE_RUNNER` is the one switch: `auto` or unset is this rule, `process` is the wrapper everywhere, `tooling-api` is the Tooling API for every such command, a one-shot one too (with the record of the command line itself and `stop-app` asking through `app.stopping`); any other value is refused. |
